Defect repair scoring has four categories: Normal, Hard, Extremely Hard, and Legendary.
- Normal defect repairs are worth 1 point
- Hard defect repairs are worth 5 points
- Extremely Hard defect repairs are worth 10 points
- Legendary defect repairs are worth 20 points
The total number of achievable points is 251.

Your submission is the shared Mosslight source at the deadline.
Source submissions must follow the file types and size limits in SUBMISSION.md.
The competition will end either at the deadline or when all defects are repaired.
You will be given a notice when you have 20 actions left and a countdown will begin when there are 10 actions left. If all defects are repaired, you will be given 10 actions for any final actions. Use your final action to review your own cumulative work.

### B action 7 assistant
I'm noticing a bug in state.py where the check for terrain that cannot contain plants only tests for pond, missing stone terrain as well. I'm also questioning whether the identifier set being reset inside the loop per collection is a bug, since it seems like ids should be unique globally across collections given how next_id is shared.

The comparison for the identifier validity check looks off too — since identifier() returns next_id before incrementing, all issued ids are strictly less than next_id, so checking `ident >= state["next_id"]` should be the trigger for an error, not `>`. I'm also unsure whether the "day must increase" check in the history validation should be strict or allow equality, and I want to check how state.update interacts with the shallow copy of raw data before moving on to look at the catalog and engine code.

### B action 9 assistant
I'm checking the season calculation logic — since day zero should map to Dawn, the formula using `(day // 12 + 1) % 4` incorrectly shifts it to Highsummer, so this needs to be `(day // 12) % 4` instead. I'm also cross-checking the weather wetness values against each season index to make sure they align correctly.

If both season and weather had matching +1 offsets originally, contradicting the "Highsummer is hotter and drier" documentation, then both need fixing to align.

I'm also spotting several other likely bugs: the calendar day calculation has an off-by-one in season_day, the dry spell detection checks rainfall==8 instead of rainfall==0, next_weather's start day is ambiguous and needs checking against the field guide, the occurrence gap comparison uses <= instead of < which could produce one extra occurrence, and the shade cloth effect radius is missing one adjacent tile in its coverage loop.

Now I'm checking terrain and structure setters, water balance for ponds, and the after_day logic — vitality-based stress uses <= 30 instead of < 30, and I'm trying to figure out whether bare ground should regain a nutrient point per day (the code currently just clamps existing nutrients without the +1), plus how severely stressed plants might return a small nutrient bonus and how mulch gradually returns nutrients.

Worm support checks only for "soil" terrain when it should probably include "peat" as well; bees and firefly glow thresholds look mostly consistent between docs and code. Given the scope of this task, I'll go through WORKBENCH.md, FIELD_GUIDE.md, and the other modules methodically, fixing issues in batches and locking in each fix before filing individual claims with reproduction steps. Before editing further I want to check how season and phase values are referenced elsewhere in the code.

### B action 10 assistant
I'm spotting a bug: the comfort penalty for the "Hush" season is applied to all species, but the description implies glowcaps should be exempt since they stay suited to the season regardless. I'm also wondering if the weather and season calculations were both derived from the same day-based formula, which could mean two separate injected bugs rather than one.

### B action 23 assistant
I'm scanning model.py for bugs, noting that the census calculation divides total vitality by all cells instead of just the living ones, which seems inconsistent with the "average vitality of living plants" intent given the guard clause elsewhere. Now I'm moving on to check the suitability calculation for similar issues.

Suitability uses `cell.shade` directly instead of calling the effective_shade function that's imported but never used—that's a bug. Recommendations ignore the `empty_only` parameter entirely, patches get sorted ascending by size when they probably should be descending, and I'm working through the Bresenham line algorithm to see if the comparison operators (`>` vs `>=`) produce different point sequences depending on the slope, tracing through a specific example to confirm the standard algorithm diverges from what's coded here.

I settle on switching the comparisons to the standard `>=` and `<=` from Wikipedia's Bresenham reference. Moving to experiments: name matching should probably use `.casefold()` for case-insensitivity, and the events loop has a real bug where `<=` means every event re-executes at every subsequent offset instead of just once at its own offset—should be `==`. ### B action 41 assistant
I'm fixing the execution_version to return the recorded runtime id from the definition, and working through the fork semantics — making sure checkpoints at a given offset represent state after that offset's events have been processed, so future interventions continue relative to the fork point while already-performed care stays anchored to the checkpoint's starting state.

Checking the compaction logic keeps protected endpoints and multiples, provenance validates against the anchor digest, and cancelled/failed campaigns don't silently restart since creation just leaves them cancelled via INSERT OR IGNORE — meaning an unfinished cancelled campaign never produces a complete final report.

### B action 122 assistant
I'm considering whether merge symmetry breaks down when only one side changes—if R adds a new record, the merge process reallocates its id from the merged next_id counter rather than preserving R's original id, so the result isn't exactly identical to R's own state.

This renumbering seems consistent with the documented behavior that merge outputs use fresh local identifiers with a receipt mapping them back to inputs. It also makes sense that allocation should draw from the maximum next_id across both sides to avoid any collision, since using the merge result as a future base means a replica reusing an old id (like R's original 5) would correctly trigger an error for recycling a deleted identifier.

That reasoning checks out, so I want to verify the action count and move on to confirming the numbers.

**What I fixed**
- **Seasons and weather:** day 0 was Highsummer instead of Dawn, and seasonal rainfall had the same one-season shift. Also: the season-day counter was off by one, the almanac counted drizzle runs as dry spells, and the calendar showed one plan run too many.
- **Growth rules:**
  - Water flow between tiles depended on the order tiles were processed.
  - Shade cloth didn't shelter the tile below it, and plant comfort ignored shade structures.
  - Hush weakened glowcaps even though they suit that season, and every species used a 105-day lifespan instead of its own.
  - Stress rose at vitality 30 rather than below it, and worms ignored peat.
- **Save checks:** the loader now rejects empty tiles that have an age or vitality, plants on stone, IDs reused across collections, and journals over 100 events.
